## 2026-10-03 — All eight search findings fixed, publication in progress

Same owning chat/checkout `logs/mobile-writing-portal-fix`, branch `codex/mobile-writing-portal-fix`, starting source `731c458158484dcdf8d06929a2c2704b5da6da94` (Sites291). Implemented shared account search in the existing Search route; Sidebar and MOBILE context Search now open it. It searches public identities by name/handle at the server before stable paging (30 at a time), with loading/error/retry/empty states, stale-response protection, literal escaped filter values and canonical profile navigation. Profiles opened from Search expose Back to the original validated internal query. Home Explore remains the world destination.

City qualifiers accept UK/GB/GBR and USA/US aliases; short country codes compare exactly, longer administrative names match prefixes rather than arbitrary substrings. London, UK now resolves; York, US no longer includes Australia. City feeds scan existing public profile reads with stable ID keyset pages instead of silently checking only the first500. No new SQL is required or prepared for these fixes. Existing private boundaries and backend/D1 resources remain unchanged.

Messages clears prior-query rows immediately and after errors, ignores disposed responses, exposes retry, and does not claim No matches after a search failure. Service queries preserve names/punctuation, merge straight/curly apostrophe identity aliases before pagination, and escape literal ILIKE percent/underscore/backslash. Matching messages page until enough distinct conversations are found; one prolific thread cannot crowd out older matches. Identity and conversation reads also page, then canonical summaries are batched and capped at50 results. This reuses existing RPC/RLS. Large corpora can require multiple reads; no artificial completeness cap or invented data.

Verification: 144 focused Node tests pass; typecheck, lint --quiet, connected client/worker build and diff check pass. Real anonymous Supabase-backed local browser verified Portalite search, canonical profile opening and Back/query preservation, London UK and York US corrections, 390/420/430 MOBILE and1280 WEB, Light/Dark, 44px/16px input and no horizontal overflow. Punctuation/empty/loading/retry/pagination and stale response cases tested with actual code and controlled data. Authenticated live Messages, physical-phone keyboard/Safari/PWA and large live datasets remain unverified. Local media proxy is network restricted (some images fall back); do not claim cover delivery was tested here. ## 2026-10-02 — MOBILE Studio workbench, source-only

Owner remains this chat in `logs/mobile-writing-portal-fix`, branch `codex/mobile-writing-portal-fix`, HEAD `04f9ea13e5cc2c18c7f8719e087e7b70ccde33ae`. Replaced MOBILE Studio inventory pills with Continue, Needs attention, Reads this week and Material. WEB Studio remains its placeholder. Drafts/Bookmarks retain separate lists; Studio context offers New project/Drafts/Bookmarks and Reads, with Select only inside lists. Profile context retains Bookmarks. Earlier source changes below are preserved. All source remains local and uncommitted: no push, publish, SQL application, backend replacement or live user-data mutation.

Active attention rules, in order: missing cover thumbnail; missing description or empty story answers; missing made_in; unanswered member page comment; member save/comment within seven days; published work at least 30 days old with measured zero opens. Deduplicated, capped at eight with Show all; Not now suppresses every row for that project for 30 days per device. Portal-field and credit rules are disabled until their actual data exists. Empty/unavailable metrics are never fabricated as zero. Continue uses the newest edit and restores Files/Details/Review from named device checkpoints; older/cross-device drafts without a checkpoint resume Files. Mere restoration does not alter edit recency. Discard cleans actual uploaded objects with recoverable progress and refuses committed work.

Added owner-authenticated GET `/api/studio/attention` to the existing Sites worker/D1 composition: existing per-page endpoints cannot provide owner-wide unanswered comments and recent disclosed member saves in two calls. With the existing flat comments model, a later owner comment on the same page counts as a reply. Recent member page actions use the existing disclosure table; historical private saves remain aggregate-only, and no actor identities are returned. One client composition combines this with owner projects, drafts, reach and bookmarks. Prepared optional D1 index only: `docs/pending-sql/studio-attention-indexes.sql`; not registered or applied. Existing portal-field migration remains pending; no mandatory new schema.

Principal changed areas: `src/features/studio/*`, publishing draft checkpoints/resume, project editor cover/story/date modes, owner draft and saved-project service reads, MOBILE context actions, page-comment deep links, and worker reach composition. Cover and story/date actions open the actual tools; Reads opens the exact page, reply opens the exact attachment/page comments, and Share resolves the existing project deep link. MOBILE story/date/cover editing uses the full-screen flow; WEB interactions remain intact.

Verification: 131 focused Node tests pass (attention fire/clear, priorities/cap/expiry, exact action routing, newest draft/steps, empty state/Reads visibility, D1 auth/privacy and legacy-table handling, checkpoint isolation and safe cleanup, retained lists/context and publishing regression checks). ## October 1, 2026 — free worldwide location directory; unpublished

Implemented the user's free GeoNames option for ALL profile location selection (onboarding and editor) and Explore, preserving the custom globe and existing city links. Search uses 235,902 cities/towns, 3,865 first-level regions and 252 country/territory entries from official GeoNames cities500/admin1/country downloads. No paid API, key, provider signup, dependency, SQL or backend configuration. Runtime requests use only own-origin hashed assets with credentials omitted. Attribution is displayed. Coverage excludes many small settlements, streets/addresses and alternate-language aliases; this is a location directory, not a replacement map renderer.

New destinations use /portalink/gn-<GeoNames ID>; legacy name routes remain intact. Exact countries/regions rank ahead of homonymous towns. Existing profile text stays unchanged until selection, with the existing 48-character limit and custom typing preserved. New feed matching requires explicit geography, applies existing privacy/hidden rules, and refuses ambiguous city labels (including ASCII alias collisions) rather than guessing. Profiles still store a text label, not the GeoNames ID. The existing 500-profile discovery limit remains. Default globe pins remain the curated catalogue; the search reaches the full directory.

## October 1, 2026 — worldwide location API research; historical, resolved above

User asks whether a free maps API can provide worldwide cities/regions. No source or configuration changes made. Async scope question pending: profile location selection plus Explore globe (recommended), or profile only. Current bundled catalogue has169 cities/92 country codes; onboarding picker uses it, while profile editor retains free text. Geoapify is the proposed hosted place-search provider: official pricing currently3000 credits/day, commercial Free plan with attribution, account/API key required; supports country/state/city/locality and coordinates. GeoNames data is a free attributed alternative if self-hosted lookup is preferred. Public OSM Nominatim is unsuitable for autocomplete under its usage policy.

Read-only integration review: existing profiles.location text can support a profile-only search without SQL if48-character validation and legacy/custom labels are respected. Globe expansion requires more than adding pins: toCityDestination, SearchBrowsePage and Supabase listLocationProjects depend on the fixed catalogue, name-only slugs collide across places, and region filtering needs explicit semantics. GeographyService is the existing extension boundary, but PortalinkPage currently calls synchronous readLocalGeography. Do not expose the unused cityFacts synthetic fallback as real data. No account created, provider key configured, dependency installed, publish, or backend changes. Existing unpublished work preserved.

## October 1, 2026 — installed-app navigation clipping correction; unpublished

User's physical-iPhone screenshot shows the main navigation capsule partly cut off below the usable Home Screen app area. Source diagnosis: the previous standalone override used a top-anchored100vh layer with an absolute dock, while useMobileViewportFrame returned immediately in standalone. A layout height larger than the visible app rectangle therefore placed the controls beyond its edge, without reconciliation on startup/resume.

Changed only platform mobile.css and the existing useMobileViewportFrame hook: the installed layer now uses min(100dvh, --mobile-dock-viewport-height). Standalone writes this new dock-only variable from the visible viewport's bottom coordinate, capped by innerHeight. Mount, splash completion, pageshow, viewport/window resize and orientation events reconcile it automatically; keyboard settling, pinch protection and cleanup remain. The low max(8px, safe-area-bottom) gap is retained. No page/root or reader sizing change, ordinary Safari/browser fixed positioning unchanged, WEB untouched. The independent public stylesheet does not consume this variable and its pending design separation is preserved.

Updated tests/mobile-viewport-frame.test.mjs (modern/legacy standalone launch, shortened viewport, expansion/resume, offsets, keyboard, rotation, pinch, fallback, restoration and unchanged page sizing) and mobile-navigation.test.mjs (CSS bound/safe area). ## October 1, 2026 — MOBILE installed-app navigation startup anchor; unpublished

User reports the Home Screen/PWA dock starts high and drops only after a touch/scroll. Source audit found the prior standalone override only reduced the bottom gap; the capsule was still fixed directly to WebKit's viewport bottom. The scroll handler changes visibility/inert state only, not geometry; navigation is outside transformed screen layers, and standalone already avoids VisualViewport measurements. This makes a stale installed-WebKit bottom anchor the likely cause, not a draggable app control. WebKit reports support this class of issue (https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=254868); the exact physical-iPhone cold-launch failure was not reproduced here.

Changed src/presentation/mobile/mobile.css only for installed MOBILE: existing transparent fixed navigation layer now has top:0, bottom:auto and height:100vh; its capsule is absolute inside it with inset-inline:0 and the existing max(8px, safe-area-bottom) clearance. No scroll nudge, delayed reposition, root lock, new JS/device detection, or hard-coded phone height. Document scrolling, dock visibility, context sheets and Safari/WEB positioning remain. Prior unpublished reader progress/slider changes are preserved.

30 focused Node tests pass (mobile-viewport-frame, mobile-navigation, mobile-dock-scroll, presentation-boundary, apple-startup, mobile-browser-surface); typecheck, full lint (0 errors/90 existing warnings), production client+worker build, diff whitespace checks and independent read-only review pass. The presentation-boundary test had stale assumptions about already-published document-level portal scroll locking and reader scrollbar hiding; tests/presentation-boundary.test.mjs now permits only their exact MOBILE predicates and restricted declarations. No related product behavior changed.

## October 1, 2026 — Home Screen blank launch diagnosis; no product change

User reported a near-white screen for about one second before Hello World and clarified it occurs in the saved-to-Home-Screen app. Read-only investigation identifies the OS launch asset: scripts/generate-apple-startup.mjs intentionally generates solid RGB248/253/252 PNGs; index.html links the exact iPhone Air420x912 DPR3 asset public/startup/apple-startup-1260x2736.png. Image inspection confirms1260x2736, one color,13939bytes, no artwork. iOS can display this launch screen before HTML/React, explaining the blank launch stage. Exact duration on the user's physical iPhone was not measured.

A second potential loading gap exists after HTML arrives: mobile preboot/React use437826-byte portalink-welcome-loop.webp without preload/high priority or a lightweight still underneath; MP4 is277347bytes and begins after React. Existing mobile static poster is89389bytes but unused on this path. WebP frame0 already contains Hello World/figure (no blank opening frames); 1500ms returning-intro timer controls duration after mount, not delayed first display. Live420px DOM confirms the WebP/MP4 and pale background; no live timing claim (the browser's read-only evaluation did not expose Performance entries). ## ALL creator read signal / Moment 3 — source only, unpublished — September 30, 2026

- **Owned checkout:** `logs/mobile-studio-20260929`, branch `codex/creator-share-previews`, HEAD `ddfded3`. Continued the owned source with earlier Moment 1/2/4/6 work intact. Root application source untouched. No commit, push, publish, connected SQL/Storage changes, or live analytics writes.
- **Implemented:** anonymous opening/furthest-painted-page collection; atomic D1 daily rollups; current-project-owner authorization for Reads; project context and Insights entry points; three summary figures, at most 40 chart bars, page detail with current save/comment totals and deliberately attributed members; page-specific notifications/deep links, 24-hour save coalescing and revision-aware read state; a Sunday-after-18:00 local visit-triggered weekly notification and a dismissible Home note.
- **Privacy:** existing saves are private. They remain unnamed. A new page-save choice offers **Save privately** or **Save and let the creator know**. Only the latter creates a named save event. A private retry atomically revokes an earlier disclosure, including when its response was lost. Removing a save/comment revokes its derived event. Author identity comes from verified profile records, never auth email. Account and target guards prevent late mutation responses or account switching from disclosing another account. Missing D1 schema leaves ordinary private saves/comments working; a named-save failure is visible and leaves the private alternative available.
- **Read/unread:** `notifications.read_at` was already declared by creator-beta SQL and used by the existing service. Missing columns map all legacy notifications to read, and missing-column acknowledgements become harmless no-ops. New D1 actions use D1 acknowledgements, merged with Supabase Activity; no privileged cross-store write or replacement of existing notifications. Individual action revisions keep a coalesced update unread when a new save arrives, without generating a new badge when an older save is removed.

### Exact anonymous request and counting behavior

- `POST /api/reach`, six exact fields: `project_id` (UUID), `session_token` (random UUID held only in module memory), `furthest_page` (zero-based, -1 for opened before paint), `page_count` (1–10000), `ref_class` (`own-link | messages | linkedin | other`), `vw_bucket` (`<400 | 400-639 | 640-1023 | 1024-1439 | 1440+`). Unknown fields, invalid ranges and bodies over 1024 actual UTF-8 bytes are rejected.
- Uses `fetch` with `credentials: 'omit'`, `referrerPolicy: 'no-referrer'` and `keepalive: true` instead of literal `navigator.sendBeacon`: that API always includes credentials, conflicting with the explicit no-cookies requirement. No account, IP, UA, raw referrer or cookie is forwarded to Supabase or stored in reach records. The raw session token is never stored; SHA-256 is scoped to project + UTC day.
- One opening request; first painted progress promptly; later monotonic progress at most once per 10 seconds. Pending progress survives SPA navigation. A real tab termination inside the throttle window can lose the last ten seconds of progress; no extra unload request violates the rate limit. In-memory tokens reset on reload, so these are **tab/day reads, not distinct people**. UI copy says reads/opens rather than claiming unique people.
- Only anonymously readable public projects qualify. Known owner self-views are excluded client-side. Actual visible painted PDF/image pages count, including mobile reading-mode current pages; prefetch alone does not. Unpaged video/audio/files record opens, without inventing a page-completion signal.
- Legacy single-PDF totals can use the existing `pdf_page_count` or the bounded viewer-resolved count. Multiple PDFs without enough per-attachment counts cannot produce reliable offsets, so page reach is withheld until resolvable; no fictitious pages are created.
- Raw events: opportunistic deletion after **30 days**, rollups after **13 months**, sampled on 1% of valid writes; no cron. Derived deliberate actions persist until revoked, separately from the 30-day notification feed. Read acknowledgements/weekly-note rows also get 30-day cleanup.
- Weekly notifications run on a signed-in platform visit/focus on Sunday after 18:00 local, with an idempotent owner/project/Sunday key; no background scheduler or email. The week query is anchored to that Sunday using seven UTC calendar dates (approximation from UTC daily rollups, not an exact rolling 168 hours or local-time-day partition). The Home note uses the latest seven UTC calendar dates and a local Monday-based dismissal key.
- Live PDF.js pages opportunistically cache their first text line in memory for new action headings. Already-rasterized page images and image-only/scanned PDFs have no text layer here, so notifications use the page number. Reach uses project-wide page order; existing action deep links retain attachment IDs and attachment/image-sequence page indexes.

Kept in **drizzle-pending**, following the existing RUM safety pattern: Sites automatically applies active `drizzle/*.sql` on publication. Do not move/apply/publish it without approval.

Complete pairs show two covers, title/year and one-line caption; incomplete/unavailable pairs are hidden. A caption falls back to the learned line. The existing project editor has a Journey-details mode for made-in, learned and the optional180-character caption, retaining save/cancel/discard behavior without touching media. All three values save in one revision-checked write; failure cannot partially commit the date and then fail the caption. Missing project columns use the existing design JSON namespaces, preserving unrelated keys. Native null clears legacy text rather than resurrecting it.
- Owner-only gaps run from the earliest dated project through the current year, inclusive. They invoke the existing guided Moment1 Files → Details → Review path on both presentations with January of the requested year as an editable preset. PDF metadata cannot overwrite that preset. An existing retained draft wins, including its last step, inputs and month; the dialog heading follows the restored month. Backfill publication does not trigger first-publication sharing/onboarding. Ordinary WEB publishing remains unchanged; WEB reentry into an existing guided draft now preserves that guided recovery path.
- **Milestones were left out** as allowed by the request. No fabricated projects, timeline entries or audience data. Learned remains `story_answers.learned`, reusing Moment1's JSON column rather than duplicating it in a new scalar column; this preserves all three answers with the smaller domain/schema change. Editing learned does not overwrite an existing prose description.

Live QR and saved PNG both have an opaque white quiet zone; PNG is1024×1024 with integer module scaling. This deliberate second tap avoids iOS transient-activation failures. The composer is immediate when native sharing is absent and always accessible through `Write a personal note`.

### Final action map

All entries below are conditional on authentication, ownership, loading/pending state and an existing callback; unavailable entries are absent, never disabled placeholders. Primary tiles precede grouped rows; destructive rows are last.

| Screen | Primary | Rows |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Home | Explore; New project | Refresh feed; Settings; Leave live session when one exists |
| Own profile | Edit profile; Share link; New project | Reorder projects; Insights; Copy link; Settings |
| Other profile | Follow/Unfollow/Cancel request; Message; Save/Unsave creator | Share; Copy link |
| Other project | Save/Unsave project; Save/Unsave this page; Share | Comment on this page; Page index when its existing overview navigator is available; Copy link |
| Own project | Edit; Change cover; Share | Page index; Copy link; Hide from portal/Show in portal (existing visibility handler); Delete project with confirmation |
| Studio | Select when selectable items exist; Search; New project | List view; Categories view |
| Messages | New message; Search | Blocked people |
| Conversation | View profile; Mute/Unmute | Block/Unblock; Report, both existing flows |
| Explore | None currently available | No available extra callbacks; globe remains reachable and interactive |
| Shared portfolio/project, signed out | Sign in; Create account; Share | Copy link |
| Shared portfolio/project, signed in | Share | Copy link |
| Message hold | React | Edit message; Unsend message only for eligible own messages, with existing confirmation; existing reaction choices retained |

## Stored covers and shared-profile bundle — ALL, source only — September 30, 2026

- Scope: user authorized source, pending SQL/Storage preparation and offline fixture QA only. Continued the owned `codex/creator-share-previews` checkout at `logs/mobile-studio-20260929` from `f007c6a`, preserving the accumulated unpublished branch rather than resetting it to older `github/main`. Coordinated subagents owned separate renderer, bundle/SQL and backfill files. No independent writer, remote push, Sites publish, hosted SQL, Storage writes, environment changes or dependency installation. Live remains Sites273 / `060832a`.
- Chosen storage model: one nullable `projects.cover_thumbnail` JSONB manifest with exact 200/400/800 WebP dimensions and hashed paths. This is smaller than a new attachment kind: it leaves attachment identity, viewer ordering, file counts and media-kind consumers intact. Browser publish/edit renders the chosen image/PDF page/video frame and saved crop through the shared renderer; PDF uses the existing PDF.js loader. Missing schema keeps legacy behavior. Missing public bucket alone falls back to private `project-media`. Cover failure after publication is reported without inviting duplicate publication.
- Thumbnail-backed project grids use only bitmap `srcset`, measured tile widths and baked dimensions. No original/PDF fallback on a failed stored thumbnail. Legacy projects retain lazy PDF fallback. Home, Discover, profile/category/activity cards, Studio project rows and index rows receive thumbnails; saved individual pages and draft/editor previews retain their page-specific sources. Public cover URLs need no signing; private cover lists use one `createSignedUrls` call. Full viewers keep originals. Shared routes use the isolated bundle cache and open only the selected project's full files; account, invalidation and cancelled-route responses cannot start stale document requests. Ordinary owner reads remain full-profile reads.
- Privacy/deletion: public uploads require a public profile, visible portal and visible project in both client and pending SQL, including service-role uploads. Before hiding/deleting, remove every public hash through Storage; SQL blocks the transition if objects remain. Project deletion also removes retired/interrupted private hashed versions while preserving unrelated original files. Pending guards, ownership and compare-and-set revision checks are documented in [cover-thumbnail-rollout.md](cover-thumbnail-rollout.md). Public downloads bypass read RLS, and already downloaded/CDN-cached covers cannot be recalled; approval must accept this limitation. Anonymous listing exposes public-visible cover paths/UUIDs/hashes and ordinary object metadata, including retained versions, never private/hidden project originals.
- **Pending, not applied:** `supabase/pending/cover_thumbnail_schema.sql` (column, validation, guarded setter, private reads and invalidation); `supabase/pending/project_covers_public_bucket.sql` (public WebP bucket, ownership/list/delete policies, visibility serialization/guards); `supabase/pending/shared_profile_bundle.sql` (SECURITY INVOKER, anon-executable explicit public JSON including kind, page count and manifest). Only an exact missing bundle-RPC error uses the old reader. Permission, malformed data and missing/private results do not fall back. No migration history was changed.
- Backfill: `scripts/backfill-cover-thumbnails.mjs` is dry-run by default, requires explicit `--apply`, uses stable sequential pagination and guarded revision/source checks, skips completed manifests and reuses existing immutable hashes. It prepares three variants and one manifest per eligible public-visible missing-thumbnail project, logs each result, and never changes originals. Offline `--dry-run --fixture` considered8, generated3 covers/9 variants/29,004 bytes, skipped5, failed0, uploads0/DB writes0; roughly0.83s is fixture-only evidence. The earlier screen's10 projects is not a verified eligible count. If all10 qualify:30 objects/10 manifest updates; reserve roughly2–10 minutes as an unmeasured planning range, to replace with an approved live dry-run measurement. Unsupported audio, oversized (>256 MiB) originals and multipart originals lacking a rendered cover are logged for follow-up; videos without a stored poster require existing ffmpeg, unavailable here. Direct ffmpeg extraction therefore remains unverified. See [backfill-cover-thumbnails.md](backfill-cover-thumbnails.md).

Bundle SQL executed twice per test run only in isolated in-memory PGlite; no Supabase connection. Follow-up hydration/metadata suite38/38 and final bundle suite4/4 pass. ## Automatic single-page zoom entry — ALL, local and unpublished — September 29, 2026

- User requested that zooming into a page automatically opens single-page view without a double-tap prerequisite. Continued owned `codex/creator-share-previews` from `14353e2`, preserving the unpublished WEB sign-in fix. Applies to rendered documents, raw PDF canvases and image pages on WEB/MOBILE, including shared views without editable density controls.
- Added `src/components/media/pageZoomEntry.ts`, connected through `useSinglePageZoom.ts` and the three attachment renderers. Modified-wheel zoom-in immediately focuses the page beneath the pointer; a touch spread previews continuously and opens the selected page at the gesture's zoom on release. Keeping original touch targets mounted until release avoids losing touch events during the focus DOM replacement. Zoom stays bounded at 1–4; controls, ordinary scrolling, inward density gestures, cancellation and the existing optional double-tap/keyboard entry remain available. Selected page and overview scroll are retained; entry starts fresh per-page scroll rather than restoring stale prior zoom offsets.
- `ProjectMediaViewer.tsx` defers its density preview until an actual inward move and preserves its starting midpoint, avoiding two handlers transforming the same page. The focused hook now accumulates tiny trackpad zoom-in deltas rather than snapping them back to fit. `entryReady` handles rendered pages arriving after a raw-PDF fallback, including an already-focused deep link. Updated document accessibility labels and `MobileProjectGestureHint.tsx` to explain direct zoom.

## Shared navigation appearance and behavior (local, unpublished)

Root cause: MOBILE project rules forced dark glass while main icons still used Light ink; later project CSS also bypassed System grey and overwrote reduced translucency. Public/share routes and preboot separately resolved a visitor theme, ignoring Settings. Stacked sidebar/structural-toggle selectors suppressed consistent selected/hover/pressed feedback.
- `App.tsx` and `index.html` now resolve all route chrome from the active WEB/MOBILE Settings record (mode, accent, System grey, contrast/translucency). Shared theme toggles write that same record. Platform preferences remain independent. Retired visitor keys are ignored, not destructively deleted.

## Full-screen Edit Profile and unified photo editor (local, unpublished)

- Scope: the owner Edit Profile experience on MOBILE and WEB. `App.tsx`, `routes.ts` and `useAppRoute.ts` replace modal state with protected `/profile/edit`, targeting only `auth.viewerProfileId`. It renders through the normal screen layers without sidebar, mobile navigation or session banner. Back/Save return through the originating history entry (also after refresh); direct entry falls back to the current owner's profile. Signed-out, initializing and missing-profile entries retain the existing account/onboarding gates.
- `ProfileEditorDialog.tsx` now exports `ProfileEditorPage`: full-canvas page, Back/title/Save header, inner scrolling and safe-area padding. Fields, account visibility, links, validation, patch-only service saves, errors/retry and profile invalidation remain intact. New `profileEditorPage.css` replaces the obsolete popup selectors removed from shared/mobile CSS. This file name is retained to minimize churn; there is no remaining modal Edit Profile component/render path.
- `MobileProfileAvatarEditor.tsx` is now the shared entry to one full-screen `ProfilePhotoEditor.tsx` (scoped CSS). Replace stages a source locally; avatar/Reposition open the same editor. Removed outer zoom/export flow. One-finger pan, stable-baseline anchored pinch, two-finger pan, keyboard/wheel/secondary slider and Reset use existing crop geometry. Bounds keep the square filled while allowing all source corners. Photo Save exports exactly the visible crop; Cancel preserves the previous pending photo; Undo restores the persisted avatar. Failed export/selection leaves the editor open. Focus containment uses dialog semantics but explicit content styling removes inherited glass, card borders/radius/shadow. It is not a floating popup.
- `avatarValidation.ts` extracts the existing accepted MIME/4MB rules; both original replacement and exported crop are validated. `useAvatarSelection.select` reports acceptance as a boolean, preserving prior previews on failure; existing onboarding consumers can ignore the result. Original source/crop is retained for unsaved edits. As before, the backend stores the final image, not a separate original-photo archive; no schema/Storage changes.

Typecheck and client/server production build pass. Source lint excluding ignored `logs/**`: 0 errors / 86 existing warnings; existing build chunk-size warning remains. Whitespace check passes. New tests: profile-editor-route, profile-photo-editor, avatar-selection; updated mobile-profile-avatar-editor and relevant onboarding mocks.

## JWT issued-at timing recovery (local, unpublished)

- Traced sign-in: successful provider authentication could be followed by a profile SELECT rejection (`JWT issued at future`), which surfaced raw technical text; concurrent auth mapping failure could also report a false logout. `authService.ts` now retries only that read with the existing session after 500/1000/2000ms (four attempts total), coalesces same-session in-flight mapping, and does not replay signup/password requests, mint replacement tokens, weaken JWT validation or bypass RLS.
- `types/auth.ts` maps that exact timing error to `session_not_ready` and plain guidance to wait briefly and try signing in again, with no password reset needed. `AuthDialog.tsx` shows the retry label and keeps entered values. `interfaces.ts` permits an optional auth-event error; `AuthContext.tsx` retains clear errors, rejects late results after sign-out/account changes/recovery/unmount and clears errors after successful recovery. Session-generation guards reject old mappings even after same-token sign-out/sign-in or A-to-B-to-A changes. Temporary same-account mapping failures do not pretend to be sign-outs.
- New `auth-session-timing.test.mjs` and `auth-signin-race.test.mjs` cover bounded retries, non-retryable errors, no auth writes/replays, stale-session lifetimes, startup retry and concurrent auth events. Included in the 158 passing focused checks above. Ignored `logs/jwt-signin-qa/` verified the actual sign-in form at 390/430px and 1280px with a synthetic timing failure followed by successful retry without re-entry. No real credentials/account writes; the production failure was not reproduced against the provider. No backend clock/configuration change was made or claimed.

## Signup confirmation recovery and password feedback (local, unpublished)

- Scope: signup, confirmation recovery and signup password controls on MOBILE and WEB only. `registration.ts` upgrades the existing IndexedDB store from v1 to v2 without deleting v1 photos. It atomically saves the validated profile fields, normalized email, registration ID, return path, accent/system grey, privacy and actual image/crop bytes before sending signup; it never stores passwords, tokens or blob URLs. Pending drafts expire after seven days and are removed only after successful completion. The existing Supabase signup metadata remains the server copy of profile text; no schema, Storage policy or Auth configuration changed.
- `useAuthFlows.ts` requires that durable save before requesting confirmation. `AuthDialog.tsx` restores a saved pending signup after reload/new tab, including photo and appearance, and restores the email-waiting notice without resending signup. Storage failures show a retry instead of opening an empty form. Passwords are deliberately blank after remount. Changing the email starts a separate registration ID, including after an uncertain request, so two pending accounts cannot overwrite each other's photo. `authService.ts` normalizes signup metadata before sending and, when a verified session has no profile/metadata, tries same-account current user metadata then an email-bound local draft. Password recovery and existing profiles are excluded from this restoration.
- `ProfileOnboardingDialog.tsx` hydrates late registration data into untouched fields instead of freezing an initially empty snapshot. It retains the saved photo and fields through confirmation/retry, blocks silent completion when a promised photo is missing, and supports retry or explicit replacement/removal. Successful explicit removal cleans its draft; unsuccessful upload retains it. `useProfileOnboarding.ts` no longer deletes saved photo bytes when input has no photo or blocks an authenticated save merely because refreshing its local image cache fails. Existing `createOrResumeProfile` account checks, existing-profile lookup, uncertain-write reconciliation and database uniqueness protection remain in place; confirmation does not overwrite an existing profile or create a second empty one.
- `AuthField.tsx` adds opt-in independent masked/revealed fields with 44px trailing Show password/Hide password buttons. Only signup opts in. Confirm-password feedback is a polite inline live region: exact text "Passwords don’t match" while nonempty and different; submit is blocked in that state. No alert/modal, password logging, or sign-in/reset-password redesign.
- **68/68 focused tests pass** across account-registration, profile-onboarding, auth-field-password, registration-draft-storage, signup-confirmation-session, password-recovery and account-entry-focus. Three new test files cover password visibility, transactional storage/upgrade/expiry and confirmation-session association. Existing account-registration tests were extended with reload/retry/late-metadata/photo-loss and changed-email cases. Typecheck, client/server production build and whitespace check pass. Source lint excluding ignored `logs/**` passes with 0 errors and 86 existing warnings. Plain `npm run lint` traverses old ignored QA/release copies in `logs/` and fails there; no lint configuration was changed. Existing build chunk-size warning remains.
- Ignored `logs/signup-recovery-qa/` injects simulated Auth/Profile services into the real signup and confirmation components, with actual browser IndexedDB. At 390/430px: picked a public test image, entered synthetic profile/email, verified empty-confirm/mismatch/match and independent eye controls, submitted, reloaded the email notice, closed the original tab, and opened confirmation in a new tab. A simulated transient profile-save failure showed the original name, country/city and loaded 180px photo. Retry saved those same values; refresh and another confirmation tab still showed **one profile, one signup request**. WEB at 1280px retained its layout, both reveal targets measured 44x44 inside their inputs, and ordinary sign-in stayed unchanged. These are simulated-email/browser-storage checks, not real Supabase signup or physical-iPhone proof. No new browser errors were observed.
- **Remaining boundary:** picture bytes are local to the browser/origin. Text metadata follows the Supabase account, but confirming on another device/browser or an isolated iOS Home Screen/Safari storage context cannot recover the local photo automatically. Asked which context reproduces the issue; no reply yet. Do not claim cross-device photo recovery. That would need a separately designed protected server-side draft/upload path; do not weaken Storage policies or silently add anonymous uploads. Same-browser new tabs and reloads are covered. No new reconfirm-profile step was introduced.
- Changed seven source files (`AuthField`, `AuthDialog`, `registration`, `useAuthFlows`, `ProfileOnboardingDialog`, `useProfileOnboarding`, `authService`), one existing test file, three new tests and this handoff. Earlier unpublished onboarding/portal/video work and unrelated untracked folders remain intact. Branch/HEAD remain `codex/splash-reliability` / `d167173ddd3f8f7021ad41de477f05f89b63e668`; no commit, push, deployment, migration or production-account/data write. Live remains Sites version 260.

A chosen-handle flow needs deliberate draft/resume contract changes and a privacy-preserving availability endpoint; no migration, API/auth change or fake availability state was added.

This limits new MOBILE location selection to that existing catalogue (disclosed during work); broader coverage remains a product decision. Native selection UI/search capabilities vary by browser/OS. Country changes clear the city, clearing city retains country, and clearing country clears the optional location. Unlisted existing values are preserved until explicit replacement/clearing. ## Mobile splash audit and continuous animation recovery - 2026-09-20 (local, unpublished)

- User reported frozen/old mobile welcome artwork on shared links and installed iPhone launch. Sites get_site confirms the public application is still version 256; the preceding 1.5-second splash/sign-in edits are not yet published.
- MOBILE now always selects the supplied looping clip, with a repeating WebP derived from the exact clip underneath it and in HTML preboot. No mobile PNG poster is referenced by active application source/HTML. `public/portalink-welcome-loop.webp` is 437826 bytes, 512x720, 42 encoded frames, 1499ms, infinite loop; SHA-256 starts `6dceb1fd`. The unchanged video SHA starts `4d282896`; both URLs use content fingerprints. The WebP was transcoded with imageio-ffmpeg 0.6.0 installed only into ignored `logs/mobile-animation-tools`, not application dependencies. No AI artwork generation or change to the supplied video. MOBILE honors the user's latest always-animation requirement rather than selecting a still for reduced motion; its exit fade still respects that preference.
- Playback remains mounted after errors, uses at most two automatic retries, and recovers on foreground, pageshow, connectivity or user interaction. Hidden forms/pagehide pause and invalidate pending attempts; cleanup removes listeners/timers. The separate returning-user 1500ms deadline and WEB behavior remain intact. Normal root/share URL fragments now allow welcome; actual authentication/recovery parameters remain excluded. Removed the unimported `src/components/SplashScreen.tsx`; retained old public asset files for cached older bundles and preserved desktop/post-preview/icon assets, working share routes and the large-file service worker.

Typecheck, changed-source lint and client/server build passed; one existing Bootstrap assertion warning and the existing bundle-size warning remain. Browser checks at 390x844 confirmed visibly different animated fallback frames with autoplay deliberately rejected, recovery to the actual video without reload, pause recovery and form hide/resume. At 430x932 preboot showed the same animation; a real local public share with `#projects` looped correctly and returned from the redesigned sign-in to playing welcome. WEB preboot retained its own poster. Existing ResponsiveImage fetchPriority warning only; no real account/data writes. Browser simulation is not physical iPhone/Low Power Mode proof.
- See `docs/mobile-startup.md` for the entry/asset map, compatibility decisions and OS limits. Apple documents that iOS can show its saved startup screenshot before the web app executes; website code cannot animate that OS-owned surface. The media service worker does not cache the app shell and must not be removed. Physical iPhone cold/resume/Low Power Mode checks are still required after publication.

## Reliable splash playback and safe fallback — 2026-09-19 (local, unpublished)

- Request: fix the splash disappearing or showing a native Play button, and explain potential causes. Branch `codex/splash-reliability` starts from the current release record `04b24b3`, preserving version 252 and unrelated untracked user folders. The prior performance pass released the splash as soon as auth initialized, which could cut the clip before playback. Video was exposed before its first frame, the timeout only called an unused completion callback, and interrupted playback had no still fallback.
- `PortalinkSplashLoader.tsx` now always paints the supplied platform poster underneath the video, revealing video only after a confirmed frame (or decoded playing state on older browsers). It disables decorative-video interaction/native controls and falls back permanently to the poster on rejected playback, decode errors, pauses, buffering interruptions or backgrounding. Pending first frames are bounded at 600ms; all playback is capped at 2200ms. WEB retains 880ms of actual playback; MOBILE normally finishes its supplied 1.5-second clip. Successful completion retains the final frame during the existing 320ms fade. Late promises/frame callbacks and queued cleanup-pause events cannot fail a newer attempt.
- `ApplicationBootstrap.tsx` restores the bounded first-intro completion handoff. Ready App/data still mount beneath it as soon as verified auth finishes, retaining the other startup optimizations. Successful playback briefly delays visible content again, by design to honor the restored intro; blocked playback completes immediately without waiting for the cap. Auth readiness/privacy and shared-route/reset-password behavior remain intact. Navigation does not replay the splash; auth retries/reduced motion retain the still-only path. Existing WEB/MOBILE media, preboot assets and platform sizing are unchanged. `src/index.css` scopes poster/video overlap and native-control suppression only to the splash.
- Verification: 43 focused Node tests passed across splash lifecycle, startup performance, viewport sizing, presentation boundary, browser surface and content-video behavior. Expanded tests execute normal/blocked/pending/failed playback, frame readiness/cancellation, pause/background fallback, legacy frame support, StrictMode cleanup races and both auth/media completion orders. Typecheck, client/server build and lint (0 errors / 86 existing warnings) passed; read-only review found no blocker. Existing build-size warnings remain.
- Browser: ignored local fixture `logs/splash-reliability-qa/` exercises the real loader under StrictMode. WEB normal playback completed in about 1.1s; MOBILE 390×844 played to 1.5s with the supplied final artwork. Blocked playback completed immediately, pending playback fell back in about 0.6s, and an injected pause exited cleanly. Still fallback checked at 430×932; WEB blocked fallback checked at 1280×900. Real shared-profile entry completed on both presentations. Only the known React `fetchPriority` warning appeared in the real profile. Viewport override reset and the temporary QA tab closed; existing development server retained. These are browser checks and simulated failure conditions, not physical iPhone/Safari or battery-mode proof.
- Official WebKit autoplay guidance confirms that silent/inline playback can still be denied by browser policy; neither agent can guarantee video autoplay on every device. The intended guarantee is a noninteractive branded fallback and bounded media wait. No commit/push, publication, migrations, Supabase/Auth/D1/worker configuration, dependency or checkpoint changes. Live remains version 252 until a separately authorized publication.

## Previous mobile Back/forward screen transitions — 2026-09-14

- Current task: MOBILE screen movement reads as travel. A left-to-right thumb swipe across project content returns to the previous screen, and marked Back/forward moves animate the leaving and arriving workspaces together. - Changed source, all uncommitted. New: `src/components/media/useProjectBackSwipe.ts`, `src/presentation/mobile/backNavigationTransition.ts`, `tests/project-back-swipe.test.mjs`. Modified: `src/App.tsx`, `src/components/media/ProjectMediaViewer.tsx`, `src/components/profile/ProfileCategoryPage.tsx`, `src/presentation/AppScreenLayers.tsx`, `src/presentation/mobile/mobile.css`, `tests/mobile-profile-workspace.test.mjs`, `tests/mobile-project-viewer.test.mjs`, `vite.config.ts`, and this note.
- Gesture: the overview swipe calls the existing navigation Back callback, not the media Close callback. Focused-page navigation, pinch/zoom, vertical scrolling, native controls, open panels, 3D manipulation and browser edge gestures retain their own handling.
- Transition signal: `backNavigationTransition.ts` holds a transient direction mark. `AppScreenLayers` reads it through `useSyncExternalStore` and, for a fresh mark, keeps the leaving workspace mounted for one transition instead of replacing the shared mobile profile layer in place. The mark never changes routing, history, or which screen is authoritative; an absent or expired mark falls back to the existing single-workspace behavior.
- Mobile CSS covers only the two-sided case, as transitions on `.screen-exit`/`.screen-next` behind the `[data-presentation='mobile']` boundary. When a move keeps a single layer there is no outgoing screen to travel against, and that case stays an instant change by design. Do not reintroduce a `@keyframes` fallback here: keyframe names are global, so they escape this file's presentation scope and `tests/presentation-boundary.test.mjs` correctly rejects them.
- `vite.config.ts` adds `server.host: true` so a phone on the same network can open the local preview. Development only; build output and deployment are unchanged.
- Verification this session: typecheck passed; lint reported 0 errors and 88 pre-existing warnings; the client and SSR/worker build passed, retaining its large-chunk warning. 75 focused Node tests passed with no failures across `project-back-swipe`, `mobile-project-viewer`, `mobile-profile-workspace`, `mobile-back-navigation`, `mobile-navigation`, `focused-page-swipe`, `content-zoom-boundary`, `presentation-boundary`, and `mobile-portals`.
- Three failures introduced by the transition work were fixed before that run: the `project-back-swipe` and `mobile-profile-workspace` harnesses lacked stubs for the new `backNavigationTransition` import and `useSyncExternalStore`, and the mobile stylesheet had two globally scoped `@keyframes`. Test stubs were added; the keyframes and their single-layer fallback rules were removed rather than relaxing the boundary rule.
- Limits: source, mock, and geometry tests only. No browser or physical-device verification was performed this session, so actual touch, native video controls, safe areas, and installed-PWA transition behavior remain unproven. An earlier browser check of the swipe used synthetic TouchEvents; its ignored local fixture is under `logs/swipe-back-qa/`.
